People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any thoughts or heard any voices that seemed real but weren't, during these periods of feeling excited or slowed down?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, not like voices talking to me or anything. Sometimes when I’m really wound up or really drained, my thoughts feel kind of intense, but I don’t think I’ve heard or seen things that weren’t there. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: During those times when you felt excited and driven, did you notice that you had more energy than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I called it being productive at the time, but yes, I had way more energy than normal. For a while it was like I couldn’t sit still—even through a meal or watching a soccer game, I’d have to jump up and make calls, sketch out menu ideas, or reorganize stuff in the kitchen. It felt useful, mostly, but… probably a bit nonstop.\n\nDoctor: Did you engage in any risky or impulsive behaviors when you were feeling excited and driven?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it risky, exactly… I mean, I probably made some decisions faster than I normally would, and maybe didn’t think everything through. But nothing that felt, like, dangerous on purpose. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: During the times when you felt slowed down and heavier, did you ever hear voices or see things that weren't there?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. When I was really low, things felt kind of… heavy and muffled, if that makes sense, but I don’t remember hearing voices or seeing things that weren’t there. I think I would’ve noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Did you ever lose touch with reality when you were feeling excited and driven?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, I felt really intense and convinced about things, but I wouldn’t say I was, like, out of reality. At least not that I noticed. Maybe other people thought I was acting weird, but I didn’t feel like I was seeing or hearing things or anything. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Did you ever feel like your thoughts were not your own when you were feeling excited and driven?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. My thoughts felt really fast, and maybe a little hard to keep up with, but they still felt like mine, if that makes sense. I never had the feeling that someone else was putting them there or anything like that. Is that what you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: During those times when you felt excited and driven, did you notice that you had more energy than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I called it being productive at the time, but yes, I had way more energy than normal. But nothing that felt, like, dangerous on purpose. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: During the times when you felt slowed down and heavier, did you ever hear voices or see things that weren't there?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. When I was really low, things felt kind of… heavy and muffled, if that makes sense, but I don’t remember hearing voices or seeing things that weren’t there. I think I would’ve noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Did you ever lose touch with reality when you were feeling excited and driven?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, I felt really intense and convinced about things, but I wouldn’t say I was, like, out of reality. At least not that I noticed. Maybe other people thought I was acting weird, but I didn’t feel like I was seeing or hearing things or anything. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Did you ever feel like your thoughts were not your own when you were feeling excited and driven?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. My thoughts felt really fast, and maybe a little hard to keep up with, but they still felt like mine, if that makes sense. I never had the feeling that someone else was putting them there or anything like that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Did you experience any hallucinations or delusions when you were feeling slowed down and heavier?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. When I was in that slowed-down, heavy place, I felt really shut down, but I wasn’t seeing things or believing anything strange like that… at least not that I’m aware of. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. After I got fired, which was embarrassing, I actually felt kind of… strangely good? Like excited, like I could finally do all these big plans I’d been thinking about. I’d snap at them because it felt like they were trying to wreck everything for me. I know that sounds like a long time, but at the time it didn’t feel bad exactly—more like I was just finally getting moving, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of feeling sad, empty, or hopeless during the past four months?\n\nPatient: Um… not really, I don’t think. I’m not totally sure. It doesn’t feel like I can point to a clean “switch,” exactly… but it also doesn’t always feel gradual in a normal way.

Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any thoughts or heard any voices that seemed real but weren't, during these periods of feeling excited or slowed down?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, not like voices talking to me or anything. I’d snap at them because it felt like they were trying to wreck everything for me. It kind of started after I got fired from the restaurant. I know that sounds like a long time, but at the time it didn’t feel bad exactly—more like I was just finally getting moving, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of feeling sad, empty, or hopeless during the past four months?\n\nPatient: Um… not really, I don’t think. Is that… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt sad or empty that lasted for two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess if I’m being honest, there has been this heavier, kind of slowed-down feeling. I didn’t really want to call it “sad,” but it’s been going on for… probably about three months now. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any thoughts or heard any voices that seemed real but weren't, during these periods of feeling excited or slowed down?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, not like voices talking to me or anything. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: During those times when you felt excited and driven, did you notice that you had more energy than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I called it being productive at the time, but yes, I had way more energy than normal. It felt useful, mostly, but… probably a bit nonstop.\n\nDoctor: Did you engage in any risky or impulsive behaviors when you were feeling excited and driven?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it risky, exactly… I mean, I probably made some decisions faster than I normally would, and maybe didn’t think everything through. But nothing that felt, like, dangerous on purpose. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: During the times when you felt slowed down and heavier, did you ever hear voices or see things that weren't there?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. When I was really low, things felt kind of… heavy and muffled, if that makes sense, but I don’t remember hearing voices or seeing things that weren’t there. I think I would’ve noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Did you ever lose touch with reality when you were feeling excited and driven?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, I felt really intense and convinced about things, but I wouldn’t say I was, like, out of reality. At least not that I noticed. Maybe other people thought I was acting weird, but I didn’t feel like I was seeing or hearing things or anything. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Did you ever feel like your thoughts were not your own when you were feeling excited and driven?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of hurting yourself during these periods of feeling slowed down and heavier?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. I mean, I’d feel really low and kind of stuck, but I don’t remember having actual thoughts of hurting myself. It was more like… just wanting the heaviness to pass, if that makes sense. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Did you spend more money than you could afford when you were feeling excited and driven?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like, going into debt or anything. I might’ve been a little more loose with money, like buying things without thinking it through as much, but I can’t really say I spent more than I could afford. At least, I don’t remember it being a major problem. Does that answer it?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt sad or empty that lasted for most of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I’ve had times where I felt down or kind of empty, yeah, but whether it was most of the day… I don’t know. I don’t want to overstate it. It was more like I’d notice it hanging around a lot, but I’d still try to get through work and act normal. Does that count?\n\nDoctor: When you were feeling excited and driven, did you take on more projects or activities than you normally would?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I did, a lot more than usual. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Did you ever feel like you had special powers or were specially important when you were feeling excited and driven?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say special powers, exactly… that sounds kind of extreme.
